| `mul.f32` then `fma.rn.f32` (epilogue) | `FMUL Rk, R2, Rk` (alpha in `R2`) then `FFMA Rk, R(17+k), UR21, Rk` (C fragment in `R21..R36` = 0, beta in `UR21`) | 128 + 128 (16 + 16 per subtile); `FADD` 0 | the contraction is already in the PTX (`mul.f32` 128, `fma.rn.f32` 128, `add.f32` 0), i.e. made by the `nvcc` front-end under `-fmad=true`, not by ptxas; alpha and beta are loaded at kernel entry (`0x0240`/`0x0260`, `0x0160`/`0x0190`) and never reloaded in the tile loop (the per-batch reload blocks are skipped for zero L-strides) |
| `st.shared.b32` (epilogue) | `STS [Rbase + imm]`, `imm = 0x200 + 0x200*(j >> 2) + 0x2000*(e % 4)`, `Rbase` one of four lane-dependent registers | 128 (16 per subtile) | the swizzle XOR is realized by `R2P PR, R3, 0x18` plus `SEL 0x60/0xa0` and `+0xc0/+0x140` base adjustments, not by `LOP3`/`SHF`; `STS` total 131 = 128 + two `STS [UR29+0x180]` (`tmem_base_ptr`) + one `STS.U8` |
| `ld.shared.b32` (C, beta != 0) | `LDS` | 128 not executed (`@!P3 LDS [R+0x200..]`) + 12 live (3 `tmem_base_ptr` `LDS [UR29+0x180]` at `0x3380`/`0x3490`/`0x69f0`, 5 `LDS.128 [R+0x160]` CLC response words, 4 ptxas-inserted reads of the reserved words `+0x50/+0x50/+0x54/+0x58` at `0x3150`/`0x3320` (`[UR5+0x10]`) and `0x4010`/`0x4090` (`[UR8+0x14]`/`[UR8+0x18]`, `UR5`/`UR8` = `(rank<<24)+0x40`) inside the alloc/dealloc sequences; no PTX `ld.shared` counterpart) | `LDS` total 140 including the 5 `LDS.128` |

Totals reconciliation with `sass_summary.txt`: `SYNCS` 254 = 116 `TRYWAIT` + 54 `PHASECHK` (52 in retry loops + 2 `test_wait`) + 43 `EXCH` + 41 `ARRIVE` (27 `RED.A1T0`, 8 `RED.A0TR`, 2 `RED`, 2 `A1T0`, 2 plain); `UTMA` 80 = 36 `UTMALDG` + 32 `UTMASTG` + 8 `UTMACMDFLUSH` + 4 `UTMACCTL`; `UTC` 9 = 4 `UTCHMMA` + 2 `UTCBAR` + 3 `UTCATOMSWS`; `FMUL` 130 = 128 + 2; `FFMA` 128; `FADD` 0; `STS` 131; `LDS` 140; `BAR` 19; `ELECT` 5; `ACQBULK` 3; `PREEXIT` 1; `EXIT` 8; `BPT.TRAP` 2; `MOV.SPILL` and `R2UR.FILL` 44 each.

Resource facts. `SHARED:1024` is the system-reserved kilobyte below the dynamic buffer: `resources.txt` reports it for every kernel in the binary including the trivial reference kernels, `cudaFuncGetAttributes` reports `sharedSizeBytes 0` for this kernel, and the alloc/dealloc sequences address that region at `(rank << 24) + 0x40..0x5c`; the B1 prediction "SHARED 0" was misstated. `LOCAL:0`, `STACK:0`: the 44 `MOV.SPILL`/`R2UR.FILL` pairs are uniform-register-to-vector-register moves, 40 of them in the never-executed C-load path (`0x4f30-0x6090`) and 4 in epilogue subtiles 3 and 5 around the `UTMASTG` groups; none in the producer, MMA or scheduler loops. 68 of the 255 vector registers allowed by `__launch_bounds__(256,1)` are used, so the vector file is not the constraint; the uniform register file is (inferred from the spills and from the eight `UMOV URn, 0x40004040` re-materializations per k-tile).

Hot-loop shapes (measured):

- MMA warp, k-tile loop `0x3890-0x3b40`: 44 instructions (10 `UIADD3`, 9 `UMOV`, 4 `ULEA`, 4 `UTCHMMA`, 3 `UISETP`, 2 `USEL`, 2 `TRYWAIT`, 2 `PLOP3`, 2 `SHF`, 3 branches, 1 `LOP3`, 1 `UPLOP3`, 1 `UTCBAR`), 41 on the fast path (the `SHF`/`TRYWAIT`/`@!P0 BRA` triple at `0x38b0-0x38d0` is skipped via `@P1 BRA 0x38e0` when the peek at `0x39f0` succeeded or was predicated off); the first iteration is not peeled; the peek of the next stage (`@P0 SYNCS...TRYWAIT` at `0x39f0`) is predicated off on the last k-tile, giving the 1 + 127 = 128 peeks per tile of B.3.2; the blocking wait (`0x38c0`) branches out of line to `0xbe80` only when the peek failed. Descriptor low words: `ULEA UR4, UR10, UR28, 0xa` (A, `+1024*s`) and `ULEA UR6, UR10, UR30, 0x9` (B, `+512*s`) then `UIADD3 +0x2/+0x4/+0x6`; bases `UR28/UR30 = 0x10000 + (((base + 0x8400 or 0x28400) >> 4) & 0x3fff)`. The peer CTA's warp 0 skips the whole leader section (`BRA.U UP3, 0x3b90`) and executes per tile only the CLC consume and the state increments. The accumulator `producer_acquire` is a blocking `TRYWAIT` on `[base + 0x110 + 8*acc_stage]` (`0x3810`).
- Producer warp 2: two rolled k-tile loops (8 and 120 iterations), 31 instructions per k-tile in the long one; rank 0's throttle `producer_acquire`/`commit` at `0x18f0`/`0x1910`; `producer_tail` = 8 unrolled waits (`0x2660-0x2a60`), the last with an inline retry loop ending in `EXIT` (`0x2a90`).
- Epilogue warps: per subtile 116-124 instructions for e = 0..6 (including the 16 dead `LDS` of C behind a never-taken predicate) and 90 for e = 7; the accumulator release `SYNCS.ARRIVE.TRANS64.RED.A1T0 RZ, [R62], RZ` with `R62 = (base + 0x110 + 8*acc_stage) & 0xFEFFFFF8` at `0xb090` precedes the arithmetic and stores of subtile 7.
- Exit sites: warp 2 `0x2a90`; scheduler `0x2f80`/`0x2fe0`; warp 0 `0x4220`; warp 3 (epi-load path) `0x6560`; warp 1 of ranks 1-3 and warp 3 for beta = 0 traverse the dispatch chain to `UISETP.GE.AND UP0, UR18, 0x4; @!P0 EXIT` (`0x6580`/`0x65a0`); epilogue `0xb730` (beta = 0) or `0xb7a0`.

PTX-level facts that the SASS reading confirmed (static inventory of the kernel, `ptx.txt`): 43 `mbarrier.init` with the Section 6.2 offsets and the Section 7.2 counts (the CLC empty count is `selp.b32 928, 800` on the C-load predicate); 64 `mbarrier.try_wait.parity` (49 blocking four-operand + 15 three-operand peeks) and 2 `test_wait`; 26 `mapa`; 19 `elect.sync`; 4 `tcgen05.mma` each preceded by `setp.ne.b32` (only the k-block-0 MMA has a live scale-c register, the other three test registers hard-wired to 1); 2 `tcgen05.commit`; 8 `tcgen05.ld`; 1 `tcgen05.wait::ld`; 1 each `alloc`/`dealloc`/`relinquish`; 3 `griddepcontrol.wait` + 1 `launch_dependents`; 4 `prefetch.tensormap`; 1 `try_cancel`, 5 `is_canceled`, 5 `get_first_ctaid`; 68 `cp.async.bulk.tensor` (2 A, 2 B, 32 C, 32 D); 8 `commit_group`, 8 `wait_group.read 1`, 1 `wait_group.read 0`; 13 `fence.proxy.async.shared::cta` (no bare `fence.proxy.async`); 6 `fence.mbarrier_init`; 17 `bar.sync` (1 x (6,160), 16 x (1,128)), 1 `bar.arrive`, 2 `bar.warp.sync`; 128 `st.shared.b32`, 130 `ld.shared.b32`, 5 `ld.shared.b128`; 5 `ld.global.b32` (the alpha/beta pointer path, never taken); 8 `%nctaid` reads (inside the swizzle path skipped for divisor 0); 10 `mov.b64 %rd, 1152921504606846976` (the cache hint); the peer-bit mask emitted as `and.b32 %r, %r, -16777224` (`0xFEFFFFF8`) at `ptx.txt:898`, `:1004`, `:4190`. Absent from the PTX: `tcgen05.fence`, `cp.async.ca/cg`, `wgmma`, `stmatrix`, `ldmatrix`, `add.f32`, `membar`, `st.global`, `trap`, `nanosleep`, `fence.release.sync_restrict`, `%smid`, `%globaltimer`.

## D.3 CLC dynamics (D9; `launch0.csv` kinds 7, 8, 9; `host.txt`)

**Native clusters.** 33 clusters were launched natively, the launch-linear ids 0..32 (`(CX, CY) = (0..31, 0)` and `(0, 1)`), equal to `cudaOccupancyMaxActiveClusters` for the real launch configuration (`max_active_clusters 33`). The naive bound `148 / 4 = 37` of Section 7.4 is not reached: 132 SMs host CTAs and 16 SMs stay idle for the whole launch (64, 65, 122-129, 142-147).

**SM placement** (measured from the `smid` column; `k` is the launch-linear cluster id):

| Cluster ids `k` | MMA pair {rank 0, rank 1} on SMs | pair {rank 2, rank 3} on SMs | SM range |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0-7 | `2k`, `2k+1` | `+16` | 0-31 |
| 8-15 | `2k+16`, `2k+17` | `+16` | 32-63 |
| 16-22 | `66 + 2(k-16)`, `+1` | `+14` | 66-93 |
| 23-29 | `94 + 2(k-23)`, `+1` | `+14` | 94-121 |
| 30-31 | `130 + 2(k-30)`, `+1` | `+6` | 130-139 |
| 32 | 134, 135 | 140, 141 | |

Every MMA pair sits on an SM pair `(2j, 2j+1)` (a TPC), and both pairs of a cluster sit in the same SM-id group. Inferred: a 2x2 cluster needs two whole TPCs in one GPC, the groups above have an even number of usable TPCs, and the 8 leftover TPCs (16 SMs) cannot be paired into a cluster, hence 33 rather than 37. The placement is deterministic for the first ids: the K0 probe kernel (grid 4x4, four clusters) put its launch-linear ids 0..3 on SMs 0,1,16,17 / 2,3,18,19 / 4,5,20,21 / 6,7,22,23, the same SMs the GEMM's ids 0..3 received.

**Tiles per cluster.** 32 tiles for 13 clusters, 31 for 19 clusters, 19 for cluster (0,0) (the probed cluster, D.0/C.8); sum 1024; mean 31.03. The 13 clusters with 32 tiles are exactly the 13 clusters with the shortest per-tile time (below).

**Hand-out order.** The 991 cancelled ids are 33..1023, each exactly once. Within each of the 33 resident clusters the successive valid ids (K5b records ordered by `state.count`) are strictly increasing (33 of 33 clusters; launch-linear id `(y0/2)*32 + x0/2`). Sorted by the issue time of the query that received them, the ids are ascending except for a few local swaps (measured; the 991 issue timestamps fall on 950 distinct 32 ns `%globaltimer` ticks, 78 hand-outs sharing a tick in 37 tied groups of 2 or, once, 6; with those ties broken by ascending id: 25 adjacent inversions among 990 adjacent pairs, every one between queries issued in strictly later ticks; 933 of 991 ids at their exact sorted position; no id displaced by more than 4 positions. Other tie orderings give 32-62 inversions, so the counts hold only under this convention). The first hand-out was id 33 to cluster (0,0), consumed 1.28 us after the first query; the last was id 1023 to cluster (23,0) at 959.2 us. Consequences for data movement: with `AlongN` and no swizzle, id `L` is the cluster tile `(m, n) = (L / 32, L % 32)`, so a cluster's successive tiles are about 33 ids apart, `m` advances by one per tile (943 of 991 consecutive pairs) while `n` drifts, and the resident clusters work on two (834 of 991 hand-outs) or three (157) adjacent 256-row bands of A at any moment, never on one.

**Slot, parity, coverage.** Every K5a/K5b/K5c record matched slot `count % 2`, producer parity `1 ^ ((count >> 1) & 1)`, consumer parity `(count >> 1) & 1` and barrier `base_0 + 208 + 8*slot`; all four CTAs of a cluster decoded identical response sequences; every valid first-CTA id is even and maps to `(tm, tn) = (y0 + ctaid.x, x0 + ctaid.y)`; the 1024 cluster tiles are covered exactly once (PASS lines of C.6 item 5). Never more than one query was outstanding per cluster (0 violations in 1024 queries); at most 25 were outstanding GPU-wide at any instant.

**Not-cancelled responses.** In all 33 K5b and 132 K5c records with `valid = 0`, the raw M/N/L fields equal the *previous* response's decoded first-CTA id (n-1), never the same slot's n-2 content: the predicated `get_first_ctaid` (`sm100_tile_scheduler.hpp:423`) leaves its asm output registers unwritten and the compiler kept the previous decode's values in them (inferred from the PTX). This confirms the rule of 7.4 that those fields must not be read.

**Query latency** (issue by the scheduler's elected lane to consumption by the same warp, 1024 queries): min 736, p10 800, median 1408, p75 1472, p90 1536, p99 1792, max 2336 ns. Bimodal: the per-cluster medians are either 768-928 ns (14 clusters) or 1408-1504 ns (19 clusters) with no overlap; the two groups do not follow the SM-id groups of the placement table: each of the groups 0-7, 8-15, 16-22 and 23-29 contains both fast (ids 0,1,3,4 / 8,9,11,12 / 16,18,19 / 23,25,26) and slow (2,5,6,7 / 10,13,14,15 / 17,20,21,22 / 24,27,28,29, plus all of 30-32) clusters; the split repeats at the same SM offsets inside each group (fast rank-0 SMs 0,2,6,8 / 32,34,38,40 / 66,70,72 / 94,98,100, i.e. offsets {0,2,6,8} in groups 0-7 and 8-15 and {0,4,6} in groups 16-22 and 23-29; measured from `launch0.csv` kinds 7, 8; inferred: position inside the GPC). Not-cancelled responses are not slower (median 1376, max 1504 ns). The scheduler's gap from consuming response `n` to issuing query `n+1` (the throttle wait) is 28.8-37.7 us (median 30.8) in the unprobed clusters, so the latency is completely hidden.

**Per-tile time in the trace build** (K5a issue-to-issue): unprobed clusters median 31.90 us (p10 30.66, p90 33.76, min 29.63, max 39.23 over 973 intervals), the first interval of each cluster being longer (median 37.9 us); the fast latency group of the previous paragraph (13 unprobed clusters) runs 31.1-31.8 us per tile, the slow group (19 clusters) 32.0-32.7 us. Cluster (0,0): median 54.35 us (52.8-65.5), 1.70x the others, the cost of its K3/K4a/K4b probes (after the caps, the `g_trace_enabled`/`g_trace_full` loads on every MMA and TMA issue, about 28 ns per probed instruction at roughly 800 per leader per tile; inferred). Timeline (times relative to the first GEMM-kernel record, the first K5a of cluster (0,0); the 32 K0 probe records of the standalone K0 kernel precede it by 475 us): first queries at 0.0-2.1 us, first consumes at 1.3-3.7 us; the 32 unprobed clusters consumed their not-cancelled response at 969.8-989.8 us (a spread of 20 us, 0.63 tile), cluster (0,0) at 997.0 us; last record at 998.7 us. K5c marks the *start* of a CTA's tile (`fetch_next_work` precedes `mma()`/`store()`; K5c of rank 0 follows K5b by 96 ns median), so the record window excludes the last tile's mainloop and epilogue; the traced launch lasted an estimated 1.05-1.07 ms (inferred), governed by cluster (0,0). The trace binary's own timed `Avg runtime` was 1.00684 ms (+13.4% over Release) with 108 registers (`kernel_numRegs`), discarded as a performance number per C.8.

## D.4 Performance baseline (D12; `baseline.txt`, `clocks.csv`, `clocks_query.txt`)

| Run | `Avg runtime` (ms) | `GFLOPS` | `Disposition` |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 0.888144 | 1.23799e6 | Passed |
| 2 | 0.888659 | 1.23727e6 | Passed |
| 3 | 0.888122 | 1.23802e6 | Passed |
| 4 | 0.888960 | 1.23685e6 | Passed |
| 5 | 0.889062 | 1.23671e6 | Passed |

Min 0.888122, median 0.888659, max 0.889062 ms (spread 0.11%); `GFLOPS = 2*8192^3 / Avg runtime` exactly. Clocks: 52 samples at 100 ms (12:40:37.0-12:40:42.1); the first six samples (four `120 MHz` at 37.000-37.311, two `772 MHz` at 37.411 and 37.511, i.e. the first 0.6 s) show the ramp 120 -> 772 -> 1965 MHz, the remaining 46 (37.612-42.116) all read 1965 MHz (the maximum SM clock of `clocks_query.txt`). That the timed loops ran at 1965 MHz is inferred, not measured: a 10-iteration timed loop lasts about 9 ms (`70_blackwell_fp16_gemm.cu:203, 416-421`), below the 100 ms sampling period, and each run's initialization, warm-up `gemm.run()` (`:400`) and reference check (`:404`, body `:343-373`) precede its timed loop, so the six ramp samples fall in run 1's pre-loop phase; the five runtimes agreeing to 0.11% supports but does not prove the inference. Memory clock 3996 MHz in every sample; power rising from 191 W to 300-308 W in steady state (303.5-308.4 W from 12:40:38.6 on, peak 308.42 W); temperature 36-40 C. Derived numbers (the `Avg runtime` interval also contains the six host encodes and two `cudaFuncSetAttribute` calls per iteration, so the kernel-only figures below are upper bounds): 1.237e15 FLOP/s = about 4.25 kFLOP per clock per SM over 148 SMs (4.77 k over the 132 active SMs) at 1965 MHz; with 32 tiles on a cluster's critical path, at most 27.75 us per tile = 216.8 ns = 426 cycles per k-tile = 106.5 cycles per 256x128x16 MMA (109.8 if the 1024 tiles are spread as 31.03 per cluster). The trace build's unprobed clusters took 31.90 us per tile (D.3), 15% more than this bound, which is consistent with its extra 40 registers and per-tile CLC probes in every CTA (inferred).

## D.5 Build, toolchain and host facts (D3, D4, D14; `config.txt`, `host.txt`)

- Compiler line of the target (`flags.make`): `-DCUTLASS_VERSIONS_GENERATED -O3 -DNDEBUG -std=c++17 "--generate-code=arch=compute_100a,code=[sm_100a]" "--generate-code=arch=compute_100a,code=[compute_100a]" -Xcompiler=-fPIE -DCUTLASS_ENABLE_TENSOR_CORE_MMA=1 -DCUTLASS_ENABLE_GDC_FOR_SM100=1 --expt-relaxed-constexpr -ftemplate-backtrace-limit=0 -DCUTLASS_TEST_LEVEL=0 -DCUTLASS_TEST_ENABLE_CACHED_RESULTS=1 -DCUTLASS_CONV_UNIT_TEST_RIGOROUS_SIZE_ENABLED=1 -DCUTLASS_DEBUG_TRACE_LEVEL=0 -Xcompiler=-Wconversion -Xcompiler=-fno-strict-aliasing`, `CUDA_DEFINES` empty. Cache: `CMAKE_BUILD_TYPE Release`, `CUTLASS_NVCC_ARCHS 100a`, `CUTLASS_ENABLE_GDC_FOR_SM100 ON`, `CMAKE_CUDA_FLAGS` empty, `__CUTLASS_CUDA_FLAGS = -DCUTLASS_ENABLE_TENSOR_CORE_MMA=1;-DCUTLASS_ENABLE_GDC_FOR_SM100=1`; no `CUTLASS_ENABLE_DIRECT_CUDA_DRIVER_CALL` or `CUTLASS_ENABLE_CUDA_HOST_ADAPTER` entry. The second `--generate-code` embeds `compute_100a` PTX in the fatbin (`ptxVersion 100`, `binaryVersion 100`); the run used the `sm_100a` cubin.
- Toolchain: `nvcc` release 13.3, V13.3.73 (built 2026-06-09); driver 610.57.04; `driver_version 13030`, `runtime_version 13030`. Device: `NVIDIA B200`, 148 SMs, capability 10.0, `device_reserved_smem_per_block 1024`, `device_max_smem_per_block_optin 232448`, cluster launch supported.
- Launch facts: `cudaGetLastError 0` after the warm-up GEMM, the reference GEMM and the compare; kernel attributes as in D.1 D4; `IsGdcEnabled_host_view 0` (host view, as documented) and device `IsGdcGloballyEnabled 1`.
- Tensor maps (`TRACE_TMAP`, 16 words each): A = `000001000d600000 0000040000246320 0 0 00001fff00001fff 0 3f00000000000000 000000000000003f 0000000000002000 0000000000000400 0...`; B identical except word 0 = `0000010015600000`; A-fallback = A, B-fallback = B; C = `0000715840000000 00000800002c63a0 0 0 00001fff00001fff 0 1f00000000000000 000000000000000f 0000000000000800 0000000000000400 0...`; D identical to C except word 0 = `0000715850000000`. Word 0 is the base address, word 1 encodes the element format and mode-1 byte stride (`0x40000246320` vs `0x800002c63a0`), word 4 the extents `8192-1` in modes 0 and 1, words 6-7 the box extents minus one (`0x3f`/`0x3f` and `0x1f`/`0xf`), word 8 the 128-byte swizzle mode field (`0x2000` vs `0x800`), word 9 `0x400` (shared). The word-level meaning is inferred from the encoder inputs. Under the Part E decision the replacement copies these driver-encoded maps out of `gemm.params()` after the unchanged `gemm.initialize()` (E.2.2), so the bytes the explicit kernel receives are these by construction; re-encoding them in the replacement (option B, E.1.2) is deferred.
- Scheduler params (H4): `problem_tiles 32 32 1`, `cluster_divisors 2 2`, `swizzle_divisor 0`, `raster_order AlongN 1`, `log_swizzle_size 0`, `grid 64 64 1`, `block 256 1 1`.

## D.6 Corrections to Parts A-C forced by the measurements (all applied in place)

1. **Epilogue smem address formula** (7.7 step 5, B.3.3 step 17, B.4): the byte term was written `128*n` with `n = 16e + j`; the measured `STS` immediates (`0x200 + 0x200*(j >> 2) + 0x2000*(e % 4)`) and the PTX offsets (`[%r75+512]` for e = 0, `[%r75+8704]` for e = 1) show `128*j` with `j = 0..15` the column inside the subtile: each subtile owns its 8192-byte stage, so the column index never exceeds 15. The derivation (`(m%32) + 32n + 512(m/32)` with `n = j`) was right; only the label was wrong.
2. **Peer-bit mask as emitted** (7.2, 7.5, 7.7, B.3.1, B.3.3, B.4): the source constant `0xFEFFFFFF` reaches the PTX as `-16777224` = `0xFEFFFFF8` (the compiler folded the three alignment bits of the 8-byte barrier address into the constant) and the SASS as `0xfefffff8`; semantically identical for every barrier address, but the emitted value is now stated.
3. **`N_native`** (7.4, 9, 11, B.3.4): the bound "at most 37" is replaced by the measured 33 with 16 idle SMs, and the resident/handout/tiles-per-cluster facts of D.3.
4. **`SHARED` resource prediction** (C.4.4 B1): `SHARED:1024` is the system-reserved shared memory reported for every kernel, not static `__shared__` of this kernel (`sharedSizeBytes 0`); the prediction "SHARED 0" was misstated.
5. **Host-adapter macro probe** (C.4.1 H5, C.6 item 6, D14): the `#ifdef` probe cannot distinguish "undefined" from "defined to `false`"; the checker now reports the macro as `INFO`. The build has no host adapter.
6. **`elect.sync` counts** (B.3.2 "641 `elect.sync`", 7.5/7.6 "elected lane issues"): true of the PTX; in the SASS no `ELECT` remains in the producer, MMA or scheduler loops because the uniform-datapath `UTMALDG`/`UTCHMMA`/`UTCBAR`/`UGETNEXTWORKID` issue once per warp. The PTX form stays the contract an explicit kernel writes.
7. **`tcgen05.alloc` execution** (7.6, B.3.2 step 1, B.6): at the SASS level the allocation atomic runs in the leader's elected lane only and the result is forwarded to the peer through a handshake in the reserved shared memory; the PTX-level statement ("whole warp, both CTAs") remains what the kernel must write.
8. **`mbarrier.init` election** (7.3 step 3, B.2 step 12): 42 of the 43 inits are unpredicated uniform `SYNCS.EXCH.64`; `fence.mbarrier_init.release.cluster` emits no SASS instruction. The PTX must still contain both.
9. **Termination order** (7.6 steps 1-2, B.3.2 step 12): ptxas emits the (inferred) relinquish lowering `UVIRTCOUNT.DEALLOC.SMPOOL` before `PREEXIT`, the reverse of the PTX order.
10. **FFMA contraction** (Section 8, B.3.3 step 16, Section 11): resolved as "contracted, by the front-end": the PTX already contains `fma.rn.f32`. The replacement must write `fmaf(beta, c, alpha*acc)` (or the same expression under `-fmad=true`).
11. **`wait_group.read 0`** (7.7, B.3.3 step 21, B.5): the instruction is present in the binary (`DEPBAR.LE SB0, 0x0`) behind the `is_producer_load_needed()` test, not absent; it is not executed for beta = 0.
12. **`fence.release.sync_restrict`** (7.2): absent from the PTX (never compiled), unlike the other beta != 0 items which are compiled and skipped.
13. **alpha/beta read order** (7.3 step 2, B.2 step 9): the PTX tests the L-batch stride and the pointer parameter before reading the scalar (the instantiated `Sm90ScalarBroadcast` has one `scalar_ptrs[1]` pointer and no `scalar_ptr_arrays` pointer-to-pointer-array member; that member exists only in `Sm90ScalarBroadcastPtrArray`, `sm90_visitor_load_tma_warpspecialized.hpp:1188-1198`, which the example does not instantiate); the pointer path (5 `ld.global.b32` sites, `ptx.txt:88, 102` in the constructor and `:2152, 2832, 2849` in the per-tile batch-stride path, the kernel's only non-TMA global loads; 5 `LDG.E` in the SASS) is compiled but not taken.
14. **`%cluster_ctaid`** (B.2 step 23): the SASS computes the x/y values from `SR_CTAID` and a driver constant table rather than reading a special register; irrelevant to the protocol but recorded.
15. **`bar.warp.sync`** (B.5): of the two PTX sites only warp 0's (`ptx.txt:1509`) has a `WARPSYNC.ALL` at its position (`0x3440`, attribution inferred); the kernel's other `WARPSYNC.ALL` (`0x32d0`) belongs to the `tcgen05.alloc` lowering, not to a `bar.warp.sync`; warp 2's per-tile `__syncwarp()` produced no instruction.
16. **Cluster (0,0) slowdown** (C.8): measured 1.70x per tile and 19 tiles instead of 31-32, not "slightly slower".
17. **Toolchain values** (Section 0, Section 11): the assumed "CUDA 13.3 on a B200" became `nvcc` 13.3.73, driver 610.57.04 (API 13030), 1965 MHz, 148 SMs, with the exact `nvcc` flag line.
18. **`SmemLayoutA/B` print form** (D1): the compiled stage mode is the nested `(1,8):(0,8192)` (`(1,8):(0,4096)` for B), equivalent to the `8:8192` / `8:4096` written in Section 7.5.

One B.4 formula failed as written: the epilogue smem column term `128*(16e+j)` (item 1; the derivation with `n = j` was right, the label was not), corrected to `128*j` from the PTX `st.shared` offsets (`trace_out/ptx.txt:3034` `[%r75+512]`, `:3208` `[%r75+8704]`) and the SASS `STS` immediates (D.2). No other prediction about a protocol value (count, mask, coordinate, descriptor, parity, slot, tile mapping, coverage) failed. Every other B.4 row was confirmed by K1-K5 (`check_trace.py`, 4105 PASS), except three rows for which no trace payload exists (K4a records coordinates, destination, size and mbarrier only; K2 records `tmem_base_ptr` only): the A/B multicast masks, the epilogue TMEM load address and the epilogue smem byte address were confirmed by the SASS reading of D.2 (`UTMALDG.3D.MULTICAST.2CTA` mask in `UR15`, `LDTM.x16 tmem[UR4 + 0x10*e]`, `STS` immediates), consistent with B.8's summary (`check_trace.py` plus the SASS reading); the mask case is also recorded in D.7.

## D.7 What the replacement kernel must target (from Parts A-D)

- **Registers and memory.** At most 68 vector registers under `__launch_bounds__(256,1)`, no local memory, no stack. The baseline appears tight in the *uniform* register file (inferred, D.2: no tool reports uniform-register usage; the evidence is the 44 `MOV.SPILL`/`R2UR.FILL` uniform-to-vector pairs, none in the producer, MMA or scheduler loops, and the eight per-k-tile `UMOV URn, 0x40004040` re-materializations), so the explicit kernel should keep loop-invariant operands few (descriptor bases, masks, barrier bases) and let ptxas keep them uniform. No static `__shared__`: the dynamic base must stay `0x400 + (rank << 24)` so that every 6.2 offset keeps its 1024/512-byte alignment and the 14-bit descriptor field cannot overflow (`0x400 + 230400 < 262144`).
- **Instruction stream shape.** Rolled k-tile loops in the producer (two loops, 8 + 120 iterations, one A and one B TMA per iteration) and in the MMA warp (four `tcgen05.mma` + one `tcgen05.commit` per iteration, no peeled first iteration, scale-c as a per-tile predicate); eight fully unrolled epilogue subtiles with one `tcgen05.ld.sync.aligned.32x32b.x16.b32` (SASS `LDTM.x16`, 8 per tile, D.2), 16 FMUL, 16 FFMA, 16 scalar `st.shared`, one `fence.proxy.async.shared::cta`, two `bar.sync 1,128`, four TMA stores, one `commit_group`, one `wait_group.read 1`; the accumulator release before the last subtile's arithmetic; one `tcgen05.wait::ld` per tile. Issuing the TMA store from every lane of one warp with uniform operands is what the baseline does and costs one `UTMASTG` per box; the replacement may do the same.
- **Arithmetic.** `t = alpha * acc; d = fmaf(beta, c, t)` with `c = 0.0f` for every element (Section 8): never `alpha * acc` alone (sign of zero), never a separate add. `alpha` and `beta` must be run-time kernel parameters read from the parameter block (baseline: `LDC R2, c[0x0][0x8a0]` and `LDCU UR21, c[0x0][0x880]` at kernel entry, D.2), not compile-time `1.0f`/`0.0f`: with constants the compiler would fold `1.0f * acc` to `acc` and `fmaf(0.0f, 0.0f, t)` to `+0.0f + t` (inferred; `1.0f * acc` is an exact identity, `fmaf(0.0f, 0.0f, t)` is one `FADD` at most, not `t`, because `+0.0 + (-0.0) = +0.0`) and the 128 `FMUL` + 128 `FFMA` per tile would not appear.
- **Addresses and descriptors.** The B.4 formulas with `smem = 0x400 + (rank << 24)`: `desc_a = 0x4000404000010880 + 2*kb + 1024*s`, `desc_b = 0x4000404000012880 + 2*kb + 512*s`, `idesc = 0x10200010`, TMEM base read from `tcgen05.alloc`'s result (0 here; do not hard-code), TMA coordinates, destinations and barrier operands as measured in D.1 D8; multicast masks `0x5 << x` (A) and none (B) from 7.5 and B.4 (K4a records no mask field; the register values `UMOV UR33, 0x5; USHF.L.U32 UR33, UR33, UR5, URZ` feeding `UTMALDG.3D.MULTICAST.2CTA` were read in the SASS, D.2). `mapa` to a fixed rank and the `& 0xFEFFFFFF` peer trick are valid on this driver/hardware (D5).
- **CLC.** `N_native` was 33 here but is not a kernel parameter: the kernel must work for any number of native clusters and any hand-out order, keep at most one query outstanding per cluster beyond the tile being loaded (the throttle: query i, which fetches tile i+1, is issued only after rank 0's warp 2 has begun loading tile i, 7.4; measured D.3: never more than one outstanding per cluster, at most 25 GPU-wide, and a query is actually in flight only about 4% of the time), consume every response exactly once per participating warp (warps 0 and 4-7 before computing/storing the current tile, warp 2 after issuing its loads; the fetched tile becomes current only at the end of the loop body, 7.4), and never read the id words of a not-cancelled response. The `AlongN` mapping `(tm, tn) = (y0 + ctaid.x, x0 + ctaid.y)` and the exactly-once coverage were verified for all 1024 tiles.
- **Exit.** Keep all four tails of 7.10 and the baseline's exit condition for the last bulk store group (at most one group not awaited on its smem read; D15 passed six of six runs); never loosen it.
- **Build and measurement.** Compile in the same target with the flags of D.5 (the explicit kernel and its host function in a separate `.cu` added to the `70_blackwell_fp16_gemm` source list, E.3.1; `cutlass_example_add_executable` passes several sources through unchanged with `BATCH_SOURCES OFF`, `examples/CMakeLists.txt:46`, `CUDA.cmake:297-301, 360-362`; the binary keeps the never-launched CUTLASS kernel because the unchanged `gemm.initialize()` odr-uses it, E.3.3); measure with the fixed command five times, interleaved with five runs of `trace_out/70_blackwell_fp16_gemm.pristine` (E.8 C2-C3), with `nvidia-smi` clock logging as in C.5 step 3; the target is `Avg runtime` 0.888-0.889 ms (`GFLOPS` about 1.237e6) at 1965 MHz on this machine, with `Disposition: Passed`, `REG` at most 68 (soft, disclosed if exceeded, E.8 D2), `LOCAL 0`, `STACK 0`, `SHARED 1024`, `CONSTANT[0]` expected 0x380 + `sizeof(ExplicitGemmParams)` (= 896 + the parameter-block size; informational, not a target: Section 12, E.5.4, E.7.4), and, on the executed beta = 0 path, the same instruction inventory as D.2 (measured, `trace_out/sass_gemm.txt`): 4 `UTCHMMA.2CTA`, 2 `UTCBAR.2CTA.MULTICAST`, the `UTCATOMSWS.2CTA.FIND_AND_SET` alloc (even CTA only; ptxas duplicated it into a retry copy) and the `UTCATOMSWS.AND` dealloc, 4 A/B `UTMALDG` (one A and one B per producer loop), 32 `UTMASTG`, 8 `UTMACMDFLUSH`, 8 `DEPBAR.LE SB0, 0x1`, one `UTMACCTL.PF` per prefetched descriptor: 4 (A, B, C, D) under the E.10 item 7 default, which prefetches `tma_c` for parity with the baseline's four (kernel `:439-445`, D.2) even though the C path is omitted; 3 (A, B, D) only if the user drops `tma_c` (E.10 item 7 alternative), 1 `UGETNEXTWORKID.BROADCAST`, 1 `PREEXIT`, 2 executed `ACQBULK` (warp 2 and rank-0 warp 1, 7.3 step 7), 8 `LDTM.x16`, 128 epilogue `FMUL` + 128 `FFMA` + 0 `FADD`, 128 epilogue `STS`, 16 `BAR.SYNC 0x1, 0x80` + 1 `BAR.SYNC 0x6, 0xa0` + 1 `BAR.ARV 0x6, 0xa0`, the per-warp `mbarrier` protocol of B.5/7.2 (one PTX `try_wait`/`arrive` site per protocol step; the SASS `TRYWAIT`/`PHASECHK` retry copies are ptxas's and not a target), and the `mbarrier.init` set of Section 6.2: 43 if the C path is kept, 33 if the 8 `epi_load` and 2 `load_order` barriers of the omitted C path are not initialized (the CLC empty count is then the constant 800, 7.2). The D.2 whole-kernel family totals (`UTMA` 80, `SYNCS` 254, `UTC` 9, `LDS` 140) are **not** a target: they include the beta != 0 instructions the baseline compiles but never executes (32 C-load `@!UP0 UTMALDG.3D`, 8 `expect_tx` `SYNCS.ARRIVE.TRANS64.RED.A0TR` on `[UR29+0x80..0x98]`, the 128 C-fragment `LDS`, `DEPBAR.LE SB0, 0x0`, the third `ACQBULK`, the C-full waits and `epi_load` releases listed in 7.2), which a beta = 0 replacement may omit (Section 0).

---

# Part E. Implementation strategy for the explicit kernel (user decision of 2026-09-10; written before any kernel code)

Part E turns the user's file-structure decision into an implementation reference. It records the decision, the feasibility analysis that supports it, the exact file set, build integration and harness edit, the contract of the host function, the internal architecture of the kernel, the bring-up and validation plan, the acceptance criteria and the risks. Every `file:line` is at commit `cb424739` unless marked "working tree" (the `dev/gemm-deabstraction` tree at 3709e725 plus the uncommitted edits of this document and `check_trace.py`). Nothing in Part E was compiled or run: this host has no `nvcc` and no GPU; every statement that needs the B200 is marked **[confirm at first build]** or **[confirm at first run]**. Part E does not repeat the protocol itself (Sections 6-7, Part B, Part D); it says where each piece of it goes in the code and how the code is checked.

## E.0 The decision (verbatim intent, then the binding form)

The user asked for: two or three files (one main `.cu` with all computation, CuTe arithmetic, tcgen05/TMA PTX wrappers and memory handling; one header with the necessary declarations; optionally one helper header for macros such as `CUDA_CHECK`); in the main `.cu` exactly one kernel function implementing the GEMM and one host function that launches it; the host function 100% semantically equivalent to `gemm.run()`; in `70_blackwell_fp16_gemm.cu` only `gemm.run()` replaced by that host function, everything else unchanged, so that the fixed build and run commands of Section 0 give an apple-to-apple accuracy and performance comparison.

Binding form adopted here (E.1 shows why it is doable and the best of the alternatives):

1. **Three new files** in `examples/70_blackwell_gemm/`: `70_blackwell_fp16_gemm_explicit.cu` (kernel + host function + every PTX wrapper), `70_blackwell_fp16_gemm_explicit.hpp` (parameter struct, the `run` declaration, the small template bridge that reads `gemm.params()`), `70_blackwell_fp16_gemm_explicit_util.hpp` (trace toggle line, error-mapping helpers, compile-time checks that need no CUDA types). File names are a proposal the user may rename (E.10, question 1).
2. **Harness edit limited to four lines** (E.4): line 1 (the Part C trace toggle `// #define CUTLASS_DEABSTRACTION_TRACE 1 ...`) becomes `#include "70_blackwell_fp16_gemm_explicit_util.hpp"` (the toggle moves to line 1 of that header so both TUs see the same macro state, E.2.3); one `#include "70_blackwell_fp16_gemm_explicit.hpp"` is inserted after `#include "helper.h"` (working tree `:87`); and the two call sites `CUTLASS_CHECK(gemm.run());` (working tree `:415` warm-up, `:439` timed loop) become `CUTLASS_CHECK(explicit_gemm::run(gemm));`. `gemm.can_implement`, `gemm.initialize`, `verify()`, the timer and the printing stay as they are; the toggle-guarded trace blocks change only under the toggle (E.7.3).
3. **One build edit**: the new `.cu` is appended to the source list of the existing target in `examples/70_blackwell_gemm/CMakeLists.txt` (E.3). No new target, flag or definition; the Section 0 commands are unchanged. This edit is a consequence of the "separate `.cu`" requirement and is disclosed as such (task rule: the user is told about every CMake change).
4. **`gemm.initialize()` stays CUTLASS's** and keeps running in the timed loop: it performs the six `cuTensorMapEncodeTiled` calls and the `cudaFuncSetAttribute(MaxDynamicSharedMemorySize)` on the CUTLASS kernel exactly as in the baseline (Section 5.2, B.1.1). The explicit host function reads the resulting driver-encoded tensor maps, the problem shape, `alpha` and `beta` out of `gemm.params()` (public accessor, `gemm_universal_adapter.h:226-228`). This supersedes the earlier plan (Section 1 item 7, Section 12 item 1, D.5, D.7 as written on 2026-09-09) under which the replacement re-encoded the six maps itself; those passages are amended in place with a pointer to E.0.
5. **The host function mirrors `run()`** (`gemm_universal_adapter.h:610-616` -> static `run` `:375-575` -> `ClusterLauncher::launch_with_fallback_cluster`, `cluster_launch.hpp:260-296`) step by step, with two disclosed deviations that the decision forces: it must additionally set `cudaFuncAttributeMaxDynamicSharedMemorySize` on the explicit kernel (the baseline's equivalent call lives in `initialize()`, `:342-352`, and targets the CUTLASS kernel), and it hard-codes the Section 0 launch geometry after checking the arguments (E.5).

## E.1 Feasibility and alternatives

### E.1.1 Why the requested structure is doable

- **Multiple `.cu` files per example target are supported without any new CMake construct.** `cutlass_example_add_executable(NAME ...)` forwards its unparsed arguments to `cutlass_add_executable(${NAME} ${__UNPARSED_ARGUMENTS} BATCH_SOURCES OFF)` (`examples/CMakeLists.txt:35-46`); `cutlass_add_executable` calls `cutlass_unify_source_files(TARGET_SOURCE_ARGS ${__UNPARSED_ARGUMENTS})` (`CUDA.cmake:360`), whose `BATCH_SOURCES OFF` branch passes the list through unchanged (`CUDA.cmake:297-301`), then `add_executable(${NAME} ${TARGET_SOURCE_ARGS})` (`:362`) and the per-target compile options and gencode flags (`:364-365`). The second `.cu` therefore becomes a second translation unit (TU) of the same target with the D.5 flag line **[confirm at first build: `build/examples/70_blackwell_gemm/CMakeFiles/70_blackwell_fp16_gemm.dir/flags.make`]**. No `-rdc`: the two TUs must not share `__device__` symbols (E.7.3).
- **The tensor maps are reachable from the harness without touching CUTLASS internals.** `GemmUniversalAdapter::params()` is public (`:226-228`) and returns `GemmKernel::Params` (`:216`, `sm100_gemm_tma_warpspecialized.hpp:225-232`: `mode`, `problem_shape`, `mainloop`, `epilogue`, `scheduler`, `hw_info`). `Copy_Atom` derives publicly from `Copy_Traits` (`cute/atom/copy_atom.hpp:52-54`), whose `tma_desc_` (`CUtensorMap`, `copy_sm90_desc.hpp:292`) is a public member (`copy_traits_sm100_tma.hpp:68, 165` for `SM100_TMA_2SM_LOAD` / `SM100_TMA_2SM_LOAD_MULTICAST`, i.e. `tma_load_b` / `tma_load_a`; `copy_traits_sm90_tma.hpp:114, 374` for `SM90_TMA_LOAD` / `SM90_TMA_STORE`, i.e. `tma_load_c` / `tma_store_d`). The working-tree probe already compiles these exact accesses (`deabstraction_trace_probes.hpp:220-226`). Member paths: `p.mainloop.tma_load_a.tma_desc_`, `p.mainloop.tma_load_b.tma_desc_` (`sm100_mma_warpspecialized.hpp:326-333`), `p.epilogue.tma_load_c.tma_desc_`, `p.epilogue.tma_store_d.tma_desc_` (`sm100_epilogue_tma_warpspecialized.hpp:286-293`), `p.problem_shape` (a `cute::Shape<int,int,int,int>`, read with `cute::get<0..3>`), beta `p.epilogue.thread.op_0.scalars[0]`, alpha `p.epilogue.thread.op_2.op_0.scalars[0]` (`Sm90VisitorImplBase<Op0..Op3>::Params` `sm90_visitor_tma_warpspecialized.hpp:1192-1197`; `Sm90ScalarBroadcast::Params = Arguments {scalars[1], scalar_ptrs[1], dScalar[1]}` `sm90_visitor_load_tma_warpspecialized.hpp:1017-1023`; tree shape from `Sm90LinearCombination`, `sm90_callbacks_tma_warpspecialized.hpp:182-213`), scheduler `p.scheduler.raster_order_`, `p.scheduler.divmod_swizzle_size_.divisor`, `p.scheduler.problem_tiles_m_/n_/l_`, `p.scheduler.log_swizzle_size_` (`tile_scheduler_params.h:1838-1853`; the H4 probe prints all of these, `deabstraction_trace_probes.hpp:231-241`, but `ExplicitGemmParams` deliberately omits `log_swizzle_size_`: the SM100 scheduler `Params` never assigns it, so it keeps its default 0 (`:1852`; `initialize_swizzle` sets only `divmod_swizzle_size_`, `:1898-1904`), and the device scheduler reads only `divmod_swizzle_size_`), and `p.mode`.
- **The host function can be a strict superset of `run()`'s observable behaviour.** `run()` does: block/grid computation, `make_cluster_launch_config` (one attribute), `check_cluster_dims`, `init(kernel)` = `cudaFuncSetAttribute(kernel, cudaFuncAttributeNonPortableClusterSizeAllowed, 1)`, `cudaLaunchKernelExC`, `cudaGetLastError` -> `kSuccess`/`kErrorInternal` (B.1.2 steps 1-7). Every one of these has a direct CUDA-runtime equivalent that the explicit `.cu` can call itself; nothing in `run()` depends on a CUTLASS type other than `Params`, which the bridge reads on the host.
- **Timing parity holds by construction** (E.5.4): `initialize()` is unchanged code on unchanged inputs; the explicit host function adds one non-synchronising `cudaFuncSetAttribute` and a sub-microsecond copy per iteration, both far below the 0.94 us per-iteration spread of the five baseline runs (D.4) and hidden behind the previous launch except in iteration 0, where both binaries expose their host work equally (E.5.4).
- **The kernel-side requirements of Section 1 and D.7 are unaffected by the file split.** The kernel is one `__global__` function whose roles are `__device__ __forceinline__` functions in the same TU; whole-kernel register allocation, `__launch_bounds__(256,1)`, `extern __shared__` without static shared memory, and the PTX inventory are TU-local properties.

### E.1.2 Alternatives considered and ranking

| Option | Description | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| **E (adopted)** | Second `.cu` in the existing target; header with a plain-old-data (POD) parameter struct plus a ~15-line template bridge reading `gemm.params()`; `gemm.run()` replaced at both call sites; `gemm.initialize()` kept | Meets every requirement of E.0 literally; smallest harness diff (4 lines: toggle line, one include, two call sites; E.4); the six encodes and their host cost stay byte-for-byte the baseline's; host function is a line-by-line mirror of `run()`. Costs: one-line CMake edit; both GEMM kernels remain in the binary (E.3.3); a template in the header (E.2.2) |
| C | Header-only: kernel, wrappers and host function all in a `.hpp` included by the example | No CMake edit, but violates "all computation ... in the main `.cu`"; the kernel's TU becomes the example's TU (the `CollectiveBuilder` instantiation and the explicit kernel compiled together; longer compile, no functional difference). Fallback only if the user forbids the CMake edit (E.10, question 2) |
| B | As E, but the host function also performs the six `cuTensorMapEncodeTiled` calls itself (the 2026-09-09 plan) and `gemm.initialize()` is removed from the timed loop | Larger harness change (the loop body changes, `initialize()`'s attribute call disappears, `verify()` no longer sees `params_` filled by CUTLASS); the encoded maps would have to be proven identical to the driver's own rather than being the same bytes. Acceptable as a *later* de-abstraction phase once E has passed; not for the apple-to-apple comparison |
| A6 | Pass `Gemm::Params` (the 2048-byte CUTLASS `Params`) unchanged as the explicit kernel's parameter, read `tma_desc_` inside the kernel | Keeps `CONSTANT[0]` at 2944 but makes the kernel depend on `GemmUniversal`/`Copy_Atom` types (violates Section 1 item 3) |
| D | Wrap `GemmUniversalAdapter::run()` and swap only the kernel pointer | Not possible: `run()` takes `device_kernel<GemmKernel>` from the template, not as an argument (`gemm_universal_adapter.h:458`, passed as `kernel` to `launch_with_fallback_cluster` at `:509-518`), and the whole adapter would remain a hidden layer |

Ranking: E > C > B > A6 > D. Six independent design lenses (alternatives, host bridge, files and build, kernel architecture, validation, timing) and their adversarial critiques were run on 2026-09-10 and all concluded E is doable and the best fit; the corrections they raised are folded into E.2-E.9.

## E.2 File set, contents and rules

### E.2.1 `70_blackwell_fp16_gemm_explicit.cu` (the only file with device code)

Contents, in this order (E.6 gives the internal architecture):

1. `#include "70_blackwell_fp16_gemm_explicit_util.hpp"` first (it carries the trace toggle line and must precede every other include so that both TUs agree on the macro), then the guarded `#include "deabstraction_trace.hpp"`, then `<cuda.h>` (`CUtensorMap`), `<cuda_runtime.h>`, `<cstdint>`, `<cstdio>`, `"cutlass/cutlass.h"` (only for `cutlass::Status`, so the harness's `CUTLASS_CHECK` accepts the return value), `"cute/swizzle.hpp"` (compile-time cross-check of the swizzle formulas only; its direct includes are `cute/config.hpp`, `container/tuple.hpp`, `numeric/integral_constant.hpp`, `numeric/math.hpp`, `algorithm/tuple_algorithms.hpp` (`cute/swizzle.hpp:33-37`); its transitive closure under `include/` in the nvcc build is 39 headers: `cute/{config,swizzle}.hpp`, `cute/container/{tuple,cuda_types,type_list}.hpp`, `cute/numeric/{integral_constant,integer_sequence,math,int,complex,numeric_types}.hpp`, `cute/algorithm/{tuple_algorithms,functional}.hpp`, `cute/util/{type_traits,print,debug}.hpp`, `cutlass/{cutlass.h,fast_math.h,array.h,array_subbyte.h,functional.h,coord.h,numeric_types.h,numeric_size.h,half.h,bfloat16.h,tfloat32.h,float8.h,exmy_base.h,float_subbyte.h,integer_subbyte.h,uint128.h,uint256.h,complex.h,real.h}`, `cutlass/arch/config.h`, `cutlass/platform/platform.h`, `cutlass/detail/{helper_macros,dependent_false}.hpp` (`cutlass/floating_point_nvrtc.h` is reached only under `__CUDACC_RTC__`), plus the CUDA headers `<cuda_runtime_api.h>` (`cute/util/debug.hpp:38`), `<cuda_fp16.h>`, `<cuda_bf16.h>`, `<cuda_fp8.h>`, `<cuComplex.h>` (`cutlass/complex.h`) and `<mma.h>` (`cutlass/functional.h`). All are header-only; the only device-scope tokens in them are the macro `#define CUTLASS_GLOBAL __global__ static` (`helper_macros.hpp:67`) and two `__device__ __noinline__` conversions under `__CUDA_ARCH__ < 530` (`half.h:191, 297`, dead for `sm_100a`); none is on the forbidden list below, and none of the four working-tree probe headers (`git diff cb424739 -- include/`) is in the closure. `cute/container/tuple.hpp` alone (the `.hpp`'s include, E.2.2) reaches 33 of these 39: all except `cute/swizzle.hpp`, `cute/algorithm/{tuple_algorithms,functional}.hpp`, `cute/numeric/complex.hpp`, `cutlass/complex.h`, `cutlass/real.h`), and the header `70_blackwell_fp16_gemm_explicit.hpp`. Forbidden in this TU: `cute/tensor.hpp`, `cutlass/gemm/*`, `cutlass/epilogue/*`, `cutlass/pipeline/*`, `cutlass/arch/barrier.h`, `cute/arch/*`, `cute/atom/*` (Section 1 item 3: the PTX strings are copied into local wrappers, E.6.2).
2. An anonymous namespace holding: the configuration constants and Section 6.2 offsets with `static_assert`s (E.6.1); the 37 PTX wrappers (E.6.2); the pipeline-state helper; the four role functions and the CLC-consume helper (E.6.3-E.6.5).
3. **Exactly one `__global__` function**, `explicit_blackwell_fp16_gemm_kernel(const __grid_constant__ ExplicitGemmParams params)` with `__launch_bounds__(256, 1)` and `extern __shared__ char smem[];` (no alignment attribute, no other `__shared__` anywhere in the TU; `device_kernel.h:114-127`). A distinctive non-template, non-overloaded name so that `cuobjdump` output can be cut by a substring of the mangled name (E.7.4).
4. **Exactly one host entry point with device knowledge**, `cutlass::Status explicit_gemm::run(ExplicitGemmParams const&)` (E.5). No other function in the TU touches the CUDA runtime.
5. Under the trace toggle only: the K1-K5 record sites inside the kernel, the hang-record wait variant, the heartbeat writes, and `explicit_trace_begin()/explicit_trace_end()` (E.7.3).

### E.2.2 `70_blackwell_fp16_gemm_explicit.hpp` (declarations, no device code)

```cpp
#pragma once
#include <cuda.h>                       // CUtensorMap
#include <cstddef>                      // offsetof
#include <utility>                      // std::declval
#include "cutlass/cutlass.h"            // cutlass::Status
#include "cute/container/tuple.hpp"     // cute::get for p.problem_shape (already pulled in by cute/swizzle.hpp in the kernel TU)
#include "70_blackwell_fp16_gemm_explicit_util.hpp"

namespace explicit_gemm {

struct ExplicitGemmParams {             // passed __grid_constant__; kernel reads tma_a/b/d (tma_c prefetch only), k, alpha, beta
  CUtensorMap tma_a, tma_b, tma_c, tma_d;   // driver-encoded maps copied from gemm.params() (A, B, C, D of Section 5.3)
  int   m, n, k, l;                     // problem shape from p.problem_shape; device reads only k
  float alpha, beta;                    // run-time scalars (Section 0): p.epilogue.thread.op_2.op_0.scalars[0], .op_0.scalars[0]
  // host-only validation fields (never read on the device):
  int   mode;                           // int(p.mode), must be int(GemmUniversalMode::kGemm)
  int   raster_order;                   // int(p.scheduler.raster_order_), must be AlongN = 1 (tile_scheduler_detail.hpp:38-41)
  int   swizzle_divisor;                // p.scheduler.divmod_swizzle_size_.divisor, must be 0
  unsigned tiles_m, tiles_n, tiles_l;   // p.scheduler.problem_tiles_*, must be 32, 32, 1
  const float* alpha_ptr; const float* beta_ptr;   // p.epilogue.thread.*.scalar_ptrs[0], must be nullptr
};
static_assert(alignof(CUtensorMap) >= 64, "CUtensorMap alignment");          // [confirm at first build: 64 in CUDA 12.x headers, 128 in CUDA 13 headers]
static_assert(offsetof(ExplicitGemmParams, tma_a) == 0, "maps first");

cutlass::Status run(ExplicitGemmParams const& params);      // defined in the .cu; declared before the template so name lookup finds it

template <class Gemm, class = decltype(std::declval<Gemm const&>().params())>
ExplicitGemmParams make_params(Gemm const& gemm) {          // the only place that names CUTLASS Params members
  auto const& p = gemm.params();
  ExplicitGemmParams e{};
  e.tma_a = p.mainloop.tma_load_a.tma_desc_;   e.tma_b = p.mainloop.tma_load_b.tma_desc_;
  e.tma_c = p.epilogue.tma_load_c.tma_desc_;   e.tma_d = p.epilogue.tma_store_d.tma_desc_;
  e.m = cute::get<0>(p.problem_shape); e.n = cute::get<1>(p.problem_shape);
  e.k = cute::get<2>(p.problem_shape); e.l = cute::get<3>(p.problem_shape);
  e.beta  = p.epilogue.thread.op_0.scalars[0];        e.beta_ptr  = p.epilogue.thread.op_0.scalar_ptrs[0];
  e.alpha = p.epilogue.thread.op_2.op_0.scalars[0];   e.alpha_ptr = p.epilogue.thread.op_2.op_0.scalar_ptrs[0];
  e.mode = int(p.mode); e.raster_order = int(p.scheduler.raster_order_);
  e.swizzle_divisor = int(p.scheduler.divmod_swizzle_size_.divisor);
  e.tiles_m = p.scheduler.problem_tiles_m_; e.tiles_n = p.scheduler.problem_tiles_n_; e.tiles_l = p.scheduler.problem_tiles_l_;
  return e;
}

template <class Gemm, class = decltype(std::declval<Gemm const&>().params())>
cutlass::Status run(Gemm const& gemm) { return run(make_params(gemm)); }   // what the harness calls

} // namespace explicit_gemm
```

Rules: the header contains no `__device__`/`__global__` code, no CUTLASS/CuTe include other than `cutlass/cutlass.h` and `cute/container/tuple.hpp` (the qualified `cute::get` must be declared at the template's definition point), and no non-`inline` non-template definition (both TUs include it; without `-rdc` any such definition would be a duplicate symbol at link time). `make_params` is a template so that the header does not name `Gemm`'s type; it is instantiated only in the example TU, where `Gemm` and all CUTLASS headers are already visible. The `ExplicitGemmParams` layout is the ABI between the two TUs; its `sizeof`/`offsetof` are checked with `static_assert` in both TUs against the same expected numbers (E.7.1) **[expected `sizeof` 4 x 128 + 6 x 4 + 6 x 4 + 2 x 8 = 576 (twelve 4-byte fields; no tail padding if `alignof(CUtensorMap)` is 64, 640 if it is 128); confirm at first build]**. Note the field order: `beta` is the *first* broadcast (`op_0`) and `alpha` the nested one (`op_2.op_0`), as follows from `Sm90LinearCombination` (`sm90_callbacks_tma_warpspecialized.hpp:182-190`: beta broadcast, C fetch, then the nested `alpha * acc` tree) and `Sm90EVT = Sm90TreeVisitor<NodeOp, ChildOps...>`, which derives from `Sm90VisitorImpl<ChildOps..., NodeOp>` (`sm90_visitor_tma_warpspecialized.hpp:711-714`, children before the node op, so `Params` is `{op_0 = beta, op_1 = C, op_2 = alpha*acc tree, op_3 = multiply_add}`, `:1192-1197`). D.6 item 13 records only the PTX read order and that the `scalar_ptrs` path is compiled but not taken.

### E.2.3 `70_blackwell_fp16_gemm_explicit_util.hpp` (host-side helpers; includes only `<cuda_runtime.h>`, `<cstdio>`, `<cstdint>` and `cutlass/cutlass.h`; no CuTe/CUTLASS kernel types, no device code)

- **Line 1 is the trace toggle**: `// #define CUTLASS_DEABSTRACTION_TRACE 1`. It replaces the toggle on line 1 of `70_blackwell_fp16_gemm.cu` (C.1 item 2, C.2), which becomes `#include "70_blackwell_fp16_gemm_explicit_util.hpp"` followed by the existing guarded `#include "deabstraction_trace.hpp"`; both TUs then see the same macro state. `deabstraction_trace_run.sh` sets `EX=examples/70_blackwell_gemm/70_blackwell_fp16_gemm_explicit_util.hpp` (the `sed '1s|...|...|'` of `toggle_on/off` is unchanged).
- **Includes**: after the toggle line, `#pragma once`, `<cuda_runtime.h>` (`cudaError_t`, `cudaGetErrorString`), `<cstdio>` (the trace printf macro), `<cstdint>` (the `uint32_t`/`uint64_t` descriptor constants) and `"cutlass/cutlass.h"` (`cutlass::Status`), nothing else. Because both TUs include this header first, `cutlass/cutlass.h` is seen before the guarded `deabstraction_trace.hpp`. This does not break the C.2 rule (`deabstraction_trace.hpp` before any CUTLASS header that contains a probe block): `cutlass/cutlass.h` pulls in only `cutlass/detail/helper_macros.hpp` (`cutlass.h:38`) and standard headers (`helper_macros.hpp:83, 87`), neither of which holds a probe block, and `#pragma once` makes the harness's own later `#include "cutlass/cutlass.h"` a no-op; `deabstraction_trace.hpp` itself includes `<cuda_runtime.h>` (`:12`) and names no CUTLASS symbol.
- `explicit_gemm::status_from_cuda(cudaError_t)` and the `EXPLICIT_GEMM_RETURN_IF_ERROR(expr)` macro used by `run()` to reproduce `run()`'s collapse of every failure to `kErrorInternal` (E.5.2), plus `explicit_gemm::trace_printf(...)`, a `printf` that compiles to nothing with the toggle off [not written as such: the macro is absent and `run()` carries a `launch_result` variable instead; the printf helper is the unused macro `EXPLICIT_GEMM_TRACE_PRINTF`; see F.2 item 12]. It must **not** redefine `CUDA_CHECK`/`CUTLASS_CHECK` (`examples/common/helper.h:43-67` already defines them and the harness includes `helper.h`); any helper is `inline` or a macro.
- A host-only `static_assert` block for the Section 0 constants that need no CUDA type (tile counts, byte sizes, the descriptor words of D.7) so that a typo is a build error in both TUs.

## E.3 Build integration and its consequences

### E.3.1 The one CMake edit

`examples/70_blackwell_gemm/CMakeLists.txt:37-45` (working tree, identical to cb424739):

```cmake
cutlass_example_add_executable(
  70_blackwell_fp16_gemm
  70_blackwell_fp16_gemm.cu
  70_blackwell_fp16_gemm_explicit.cu      # <- the only addition; must precede TEST_COMMAND_OPTIONS so that
  TEST_COMMAND_OPTIONS                     #    cmake_parse_arguments leaves it in __UNPARSED_ARGUMENTS (examples/CMakeLists.txt:39-40)
  ...
```

The Section 0 command re-runs `cmake -S . -B build ...` and picks the change up; the target name, its flags, its link line (`CUTLASS`, `cutlass_tools_util_includes`, `cuda`; `examples/CMakeLists.txt:50-57`) and include directories (`:59-64`) are unchanged.

### E.3.2 Two translation units

- Each `.cu` is compiled to its own object with its own fatbin (`sm_100a` cubin + `compute_100a` PTX, D.5). `cuobjdump --list-elf` on the linked binary therefore lists **two** `sm_100a` cubins (the baseline's `elf.txt` shows one), and `--dump-resource-usage`/`--dump-sass`/`--dump-ptx` print two ELF sections **[confirm at first build]**. Every inspection step cuts the explicit kernel's function by name (E.7.4); never count mnemonics over the whole file.
- No `-rdc`: a `__device__` variable or `__global__` function defined in a header included by both TUs produces two independent copies, and a non-`static` one produces duplicate host-side shadow symbols at link time **[inferred from nvcc whole-program semantics; confirm at the first toggle-on build]**. Consequence for the trace headers: E.7.3.

### E.3.3 Both GEMM kernels remain in the binary

`gemm.initialize()` passes `device_kernel<GemmKernel>` to `cudaFuncSetAttribute` (`gemm_universal_adapter.h:344-347`), an odr-use that instantiates the CUTLASS kernel into the example TU's fatbin, and `can_implement`/`initialize` instantiate the host side of `GemmUniversal`. The CUTLASS kernel is therefore compiled, present and never launched. Effects: build time and binary size grow (irrelevant to the timed interval); the per-iteration attribute call on it is the baseline's own call (E.5.4); its SASS must be unchanged from `trace_out/sass_gemm.txt` (a cheap check that the CUTLASS headers were not modified); under lazy module loading (default since CUDA 12.2, **inferred**) its module loads at the first `initialize()` (working tree `:410`) and the explicit kernel's module at the warm-up call (`:416`), both before `timer.start()` (`:436`).

### E.3.4 ctest side effect

Building the examples (`CUTLASS_ENABLE_EXAMPLES=ON`, top-level `CMakeLists.txt:1143-1144`) registers four ctest entries for this target through `cutlass_add_executable_tests` (called unconditionally at `examples/CMakeLists.txt:71-78`, defined at top-level `CMakeLists.txt:865`; `enable_testing()` at `:809` is unconditional, so the registration does not depend on `CUTLASS_ENABLE_TESTS`), one per `TEST_COMMAND_OPTIONS` variable: `--swizzle=1`, `--swizzle=2`, `--swizzle=5`, `--swizzle=5 --m=4096 --n=16384` (`CMakeLists.txt:31-34`). The explicit kernel supports only the Section 0 configuration. With the guards of E.5.1 the `--swizzle=2`, `--swizzle=5` and uneven-shape entries fail loudly (`Got CUTLASS error ... Error Invalid Problem`) instead of computing a wrong problem; `--swizzle=1` behaves as swizzle 0 in the baseline scheduler (`divmod_swizzle_size_.divisor` becomes 0 for `max_swizzle_size <= 1`, `tile_scheduler_params.h:1898-1904`) and passes the guard. The fixed commands never run ctest; the user is informed (E.10, question 4).

## E.4 The harness after the edit (`70_blackwell_fp16_gemm.cu`, working tree)

| Line(s) | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `// #define CUTLASS_DEABSTRACTION_TRACE 1  // ...` | `#include "70_blackwell_fp16_gemm_explicit_util.hpp"` (the toggle is now line 1 of that header; C.1 item 2 amended) |
| 2-4 | guarded `#include "deabstraction_trace.hpp"` | unchanged |
| 87 | `#include "helper.h"` | unchanged, followed by `#include "70_blackwell_fp16_gemm_explicit.hpp"` |
| 415 | `CUTLASS_CHECK(gemm.run());` | `CUTLASS_CHECK(explicit_gemm::run(gemm));` |
| 439 | `CUTLASS_CHECK(gemm.run());` | `CUTLASS_CHECK(explicit_gemm::run(gemm));` |

Everything else is untouched: `Options`, data fill, `gemm.can_implement`, `gemm.initialize` at `:410` and `:438`, `verify()` at `:420`, `Disposition`, `GpuTimer`, the printing. Both call sites must change: replacing only the timed one would make `verify()` validate the CUTLASS kernel's output and put the explicit kernel's module load and first launch inside the timed interval. Consequence for messages: a failing warm-up prints `Got CUTLASS error while calling explicit_gemm::run(gemm) at line <N>: <status string>` (the expression text changes, and the line number differs from the baseline's `:400`/`:419`: the working tree at HEAD 0ddb03fc read `:415`/`:439` because of the committed Part C trace blocks; the include inserted after `:87` moves the warm-up call to `:416` while the timed call stays at `:439` because the Part C guarded block between them was re-pointed one line shorter (E.7.3); the line-1 edit is one-for-one and shifts nothing; match on the text, not the number). Under the toggle, the guarded calls of C.2 (`trace_reset`, `trace_probe_kernel_k0`, `trace_host_probes<Gemm>`, `trace_host_post_run`, `trace_dump`, `trace_disable`) are re-pointed as E.7.3 describes.

## E.5 The host function: contract and sequence

### E.5.1 Guards (before any CUDA call)

`run(ExplicitGemmParams const&)` first checks that the parameters describe the configuration the kernel hard-codes and returns a `cutlass::Status` without launching otherwise (the device code contains **no** checks, so that its instruction inventory is not changed; the host function is the only guard):

| Check | Expected | Status if violated |
|---|---|---|
| `mode` | `kGemm` (0) | `kErrorInvalidProblem` |
| `m, n, k, l` | 8192, 8192, 8192, 1 (`k % 64 == 0`, full 128x128 tiles, `L = 1`) | `kErrorInvalidProblem` |
| `raster_order` / `swizzle_divisor` | `AlongN` = **1** / 0 (`enum class RasterOrder { AlongM, AlongN }`, `tile_scheduler_detail.hpp:38-41`, so `AlongM` = 0, `AlongN` = 1; the kernel TU cannot name the enumerator because E.2.1 forbids `cutlass/gemm/*`, so it compares against the literal 1; measured `scheduler_raster_order AlongN 1`, D.5 H4) | `kErrorInvalidProblem` |
| `tiles_m, tiles_n, tiles_l` | 32, 32, 1 (consistency with `m, n, l`) | `kErrorInvalidProblem` |
| `alpha_ptr`, `beta_ptr` | both `nullptr` (the pointer path is compiled but not taken in the baseline, D.6 item 13; the explicit kernel has no pointer path) | `kErrorNotSupported` |
| `alpha` | not checked: a run-time kernel parameter (Section 0, E.2.2); any value is computed as `alpha * acc` exactly as in the baseline | - |
| `beta` | must satisfy `beta == 0.0f` (IEEE comparison, so `-0.0f` passes, matching the baseline's `is_zero()` test, Section 8). The C path is omitted, so any other value would give a wrong `D`; E.10 item 5 default: reject loudly (alternative there: run anyway and document the wrong `D`) | `kErrorNotSupported` for `beta != 0.0f` |

These statuses print through `CUTLASS_CHECK` (`examples/common/helper.h:43-52`) as `Error Invalid Problem` / `Error Not Supported` (`cutlass.h:47-60` for the enumerators; `cutlassGetStatusString` `:64-92`, strings at `:74-77`). The baseline's `run()` has no such guards (they live in `can_implement`, which the harness still calls on the CUTLASS side); the extra checks are a documented deviation that only affects arguments outside Section 0.

### E.5.2 The `run()` mirror (every call of the host function, in this order)

| # | Explicit host function | Mirrors | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `cudaFuncSetAttribute(explicit_kernel, cudaFuncAttributeMaxDynamicSharedMemorySize, 230400)` | `initialize()` `:342-352` (on the CUTLASS kernel) | **Required**: a launch with 230400 dynamic bytes fails without the opt-in (default limit 48 KiB); the baseline's call targets the other kernel. Performed on every call (not hoisted) so that the number of attribute calls on the *launched* kernel per iteration stays two, as in the baseline (E.5.4) |
| 2 | `grid = dim3(64, 64, 1)`, `block = dim3(256, 1, 1)`, `smem = 230400` | `run()` `:380-384` -> `get_grid_shape` (`sm100_gemm_tma_warpspecialized.hpp:383-396` -> `sm100_tile_scheduler.hpp:220-250`, `possibly_transpose_grid` for `AlongN`) | Hard-coded after the E.5.1 guards; equal to the baseline's `(N/128, M/128, 1)` for the fixed shape (B.1.2 step 2) |
| 3 | `check_cluster_dims(grid, {2,2,1})`: `4 <= 32`, `64 % 2 == 0` | `cluster_launch.hpp:103-112` via `:274-277` | returns `kInvalid` -> collapsed to `kErrorInternal` as `run()` does |
| 4 | `cudaFuncSetAttribute(explicit_kernel, cudaFuncAttributeNonPortableClusterSizeAllowed, 1)` | `ClusterLauncher::init` `:141-143`, called from `:280` on every launch | functionally irrelevant for a 4-CTA cluster (portable maximum 8, **inferred**); kept for parity |
| 5 | `cudaLaunchConfig_t cfg{grid, block, 230400, /*stream*/ nullptr, attrs, /*numAttrs*/ 1}` with `attrs[0] = {cudaLaunchAttributeClusterDimension, {2,2,1}}` | `make_cluster_launch_config` `:151-213` (`numAttrs` 3 -> 2 -> 1: no fallback, no PDL; B.1.2 step 4); stream `nullptr` = legacy default stream, the stream `GpuTimer` records on | the same configuration `trace_host_post_run` uses for `cudaOccupancyMaxActiveClusters` (working tree `deabstraction_trace_probes.hpp:319-331`) |
| 6 | `void* args[] = {const_cast<ExplicitGemmParams*>(&params)}; cudaLaunchKernelExC(&cfg, reinterpret_cast<void const*>(&explicit_kernel), args)` | `:293-294` (`synclog_setup()` is a no-op without `CUTLASS_ENABLE_SYNCLOG`, D.5) | asynchronous; copies `sizeof(ExplicitGemmParams)` bytes into the parameter buffer (the baseline copies 2048) |
| 7 | `cudaError_t e = cudaGetLastError(); return (e == cudaSuccess && every step above succeeded) ? kSuccess : kErrorInternal;` | `run()` `:564-574` | The rejection itself is already reported by the return value of `cudaLaunchKernelExC` (item 6, mapped to `kErrorInternal` through the `launch_result` if-chain; F.2 item 12); item 7 mirrors `run()` and clears the per-thread error variable so that a stale non-sticky error is not picked up by a later `cudaGetLastError` (e.g. `trace_host_post_run`, working tree `deabstraction_trace_probes.hpp:304`), and it keeps the per-iteration host call sequence of E.5.4 identical in kind to the baseline's. A host-side launch rejection is not returned by `cudaDeviceSynchronize`; if it were left unreported the symptom would be `Disposition: Failed` (D never written), not the E.7.2 'Device fault' row; a genuine `Got CUDA error while calling cudaDeviceSynchronize()` in `verify()` is a device-side fault (E.7.2) [inferred; confirm at first run]. Under the toggle the function prints `cudaGetErrorString(e)` (`TRACE_HOST explicit_run_launch_error`) only when `e` or a step's `launch_result` failed, not before every return (F.2 item 15) |

Not in the function (and not allowed in the timed path): `cudaDeviceSynchronize`, `cudaStreamSynchronize`, `cudaMemcpy`, `cudaFuncGetAttributes`, `cudaOccupancyMaxActiveClusters`, any `printf` with the toggle off, any second launch.

### E.5.3 What "100% semantically equivalent to `gemm.run()`" means here, and the two deviations

Equivalent: same stream, same launch API, same launch configuration (grid, block, dynamic smem, one cluster attribute), same attribute call on the launched kernel (`NonPortableClusterSizeAllowed`), same return-status mapping, same asynchrony, the same `params_` snapshot (the host function reads `gemm.params()` as left by the preceding `gemm.initialize()`, exactly what `run()` reads through `params_`, `:615`). Deviations forced by the decision, to be stated in the deliverable: (a) the additional `MaxDynamicSharedMemorySize` attribute call on the explicit kernel (item 1); (b) the E.5.1 guards and the hard-coded grid instead of `get_grid_shape(params)` (Section 0 permits hard-coding; the guards make the restriction explicit).

### E.5.4 Timed-interval accounting (why the comparison stays apple-to-apple)

- `GpuTimer` (`examples/common/helper.h:72-113`) records `start`/`stop` events on stream 0; `elapsed_millis()` is the GPU-side span between them: exposed host latency before kernel 0 + ten kernel durations + nine inter-kernel gaps. The GPU is idle at `timer.start()` because `verify()` ends with `cudaDeviceSynchronize()` (working tree `:374`) and `BlockCompareEqual` drains the stream (`tensor_compare.h:152-158`), so **iteration 0's host work is exposed in both binaries**; iterations 1-9 hide their host work behind the ~0.888 ms kernel as long as it is far shorter, which 22-23 non-synchronising calls are (**inferred**; B.1.2 step 7 records that this was not measured).
- Per-iteration host sequence, baseline: `initialize()` = 6 x {`cudaGetDriverEntryPointByVersion`, `cuTensorMapEncodeTiled`, `cudaDriverGetVersion`} + `cudaFuncSetAttribute(MaxDynamicSmem)` on the CUTLASS kernel; `run()` = `cudaFuncSetAttribute(NonPortable)` + `cudaLaunchKernelExC` + `cudaGetLastError`: 22 calls, 2 attribute calls on the launched kernel. Replacement: the same `initialize()` (19 calls, unchanged code) + E.5.2 items 1, 4, 6, 7: 23 calls, 3 attribute calls of which 2 on the launched kernel. Net: +1 host-only `cudaFuncSetAttribute` (low microseconds, **inferred**) + one host copy of `sizeof(ExplicitGemmParams)` bytes, per iteration, hidden except in iteration 0, i.e. at most a few microseconds once per process against a 0.94 us per-iteration baseline spread (D.4) and a ~0.5 us `cudaEventElapsedTime` floor (**inferred**).
- Hoisting item 1 behind a `static bool` would save nothing measurable and would make the per-iteration sequence differ from the baseline in kind (the launched kernel would receive 1 attribute call instead of the baseline's 2; the per-iteration totals would match, 2 vs 2, only by counting `initialize()`'s call on the never-launched CUTLASS kernel, which keeps receiving it either way); if the implementer nevertheless hoists it, Section 12 item 2 (`nsys` kernel-only durations) becomes mandatory. `nsys profile --trace=cuda` also records every runtime API call and is the direct way to verify the "22 vs 23, none synchronising" statement (optional, outside the fixed command).
- Kernel-side factors that legitimately differ and are the object of the comparison: the SASS itself (REG, loop shapes), the parameter block size (`CONSTANT[0]` = 0x380 + `sizeof(ExplicitGemmParams)` instead of 2944; parameters are read in the prologue and once per tile, never in the k-tile loops), the drain of the last CTA (tails, dealloc, `PREEXIT`) and the next kernel's cluster scheduling inside each inter-kernel gap. Identical by construction: L2/DRAM state (same buffers, addresses, tile mapping and CLC protocol), occupancy (`N_native = 33` for any `REG <= 255` at 256 threads, **inferred**), the absence of PDL (`griddepcontrol.wait` returns immediately without the attribute, **inferred**).

## E.6 Kernel architecture inside the `.cu`

### E.6.1 Constants, offsets, descriptors (all `constexpr`, with `static_assert`s)

```cpp
constexpr uint32_t kThreads = 256;  constexpr int kCtaTileM = 128, kCtaTileN = 128, kTileK = 64;
constexpr int kMmaM = 256, kMmaN = 128, kMmaK = 16, kKBlocks = 4;          // one tcgen05.mma.cta_group::2 per k-block, 4 per k-tile
constexpr int kStages = 8, kAccStages = 4, kClcStages = 2, kThrottleStages = 2, kEpiStages = 4, kEpiSubtiles = 8, kEpiTileN = 16;
constexpr uint32_t kTmemColumns = 512;  constexpr int kPrologueKTiles = 8;   // first load() call covers min(8, k_tiles)
// Section 6.2 byte offsets from the dynamic smem base
constexpr uint32_t OFF_MAIN_FULL = 0, OFF_MAIN_EMPTY = 64, OFF_EPI_LOAD_FULL = 128, OFF_EPI_LOAD_EMPTY = 160, OFF_LOAD_ORDER = 192,
  OFF_CLC_FULL = 208, OFF_CLC_EMPTY = 224, OFF_ACC_FULL = 240, OFF_ACC_EMPTY = 272, OFF_THROTTLE_FULL = 304, OFF_THROTTLE_EMPTY = 320,
  OFF_TMEM_DEALLOC = 336, OFF_CLC_RESPONSE = 352, OFF_TMEM_BASE_PTR = 384, OFF_SMEM_CD = 512, OFF_SMEM_A = 33792, OFF_SMEM_B = 164864;
constexpr uint32_t kSmemBytes = 230400;
static_assert(OFF_SMEM_A == OFF_SMEM_CD + kEpiStages*8192 + 512 && OFF_SMEM_B == OFF_SMEM_A + kStages*16384 && kSmemBytes == OFF_SMEM_B + kStages*8192, "6.2");
static_assert(OFF_SMEM_A % 1024 == 0 && OFF_SMEM_B % 1024 == 0 && OFF_SMEM_CD % 512 == 0 && OFF_CLC_RESPONSE % 16 == 0, "alignments");
static_assert(0x400 + kSmemBytes < (1u << 18), "14-bit UMMA start-address field");
// descriptors (D.7): hi word constant, lo word = LBO(1) | start_address>>4 (14 bits, rank bits dropped)
constexpr uint32_t kSmemDescHi = 0x40004040u, kSmemDescLo0 = 0x00010000u, kInstrDesc = 0x10200010u;
static_assert((64u | (1u << 14) | (2u << 29)) == kSmemDescHi && ((1u << 4) | (16u << 17) | (16u << 24)) == kInstrDesc, "mma_sm100_desc.hpp:101-127, 416-443");
// protocol constants (7.2)
constexpr uint32_t kMainloopTxBytes = 49152, kClcTxBytes = 16, kMainFullCount = 1, kMainEmptyCount = 2, kClcFullCount = 1, kClcEmptyCount = 800,
  kAccFullCount = 1, kAccEmptyCount = 256, kThrottleCount = 32, kTmemDeallocCount = 32, kPeerBitMask = 0xFEFFFFFFu, kWaitTicks = 0x989680u;
constexpr uint16_t kMainloopReleaseMask = 0xF;  constexpr uint64_t kCacheHintEvictNormal = 0x1000000000000000ull;
constexpr uint32_t kBarEpilogue = 1, kBarTmemAlloc = 6, kEpilogueThreads = 128, kTmemAllocBarThreads = 160;
```

Run-time, warp-uniform: `acc_commit_mask = uint16_t(0x3 << (2*y))`, `tma_a_mcast_mask = uint16_t(0x5 << x)`, `(x, y) = %cluster_ctaid`, `rank = %cluster_ctarank = x + 2y`, `is_leader = (rank & 1) == 0`, `peer = rank ^ 1`, `is_cta0 = rank == 0` (7.1). The offsets are only semantically required (6.2), but keeping the measured values gives address parity with K1/K4 and lets `check_trace.py` run unchanged (E.7.3). The dynamic base is **computed** (`smem_u32(smem)` = `__cvta_generic_to_shared`) and only *checked* against `0x400 + (rank << 24)` under the toggle; it must not be hard-coded. The TMEM base is read from `smem[OFF_TMEM_BASE_PTR]` after `tcgen05.alloc` (measured 0, D6; never hard-coded).

CuTe usage (Section 1 item 4), compile time only: `static_assert(cute::Swizzle<2,5,2>::yyy_msk::value == 0x180 && cute::Swizzle<2,5,2>::num_shft == 2)` and, if constant-evaluable **[confirm at first build]**, `static_assert(epi_swz(0x1E4) == cute::Swizzle<2,5,2>{}(0x1E4u))` for the epilogue formula `epi_swz(o) = o ^ ((o & 0x180) >> 2)`; `Swizzle<3,4,3>` is only documented (TMA applies it on write, UMMA on read; the kernel never computes it). No `cute::Tensor`, `Layout`, `TiledMma`, `TiledCopy`, `Copy_Atom`, `PipelineState`, `cute::copy`/`gemm` anywhere in device code; coordinates are `int` arithmetic on `(tm, tn, q, s, e, i, j)`.

**Address-space rule, stated once:** every `mbarrier.*`, `cp.async.bulk.tensor.*`, `tcgen05.*`, `clusterlaunchcontrol.*`, `mapa` operand is a 32-bit shared address `smem_base + OFF_*` (`"r"`); every C++ dereference (the `T` read, the epilogue `float` stores if written in C++) uses the generic pointer `smem + OFF_*`. Mixing them (indexing a generic `float*` by a `shared::cta` address) is off by 1 KiB in rank 0 and by 16-48 MiB in ranks 1-3. The epilogue stores are written either as `reinterpret_cast<float*>(smem + OFF_SMEM_CD)[...]` (no `smem_base` term) or as `asm volatile("st.shared.b32 [%0], %1;" :: "r"(addr), "f"(d) : "memory")` on the 32-bit address; the first form is preferred, the second is the fallback if the PTX shows generic `st.b32` **[confirm at first build]**.

### E.6.2 PTX wrapper inventory (37 wrappers, each one `asm volatile` copied from the cited source)

All are `__device__ __forceinline__` free functions in the anonymous namespace. Constraint letters: `"r"` 32-bit, `"l"` 64-bit, `"h"` 16-bit, `"n"` immediate, `"f"` float. The `"memory"` clobbers follow the source exactly (present on every `mbarrier.*` wait/arrive/expect_tx and every fence with `barrier.h` origin, on the named barriers `bar.sync`/`bar.arrive` (W18/W19, `barrier.h:287, 308`), `tcgen05.commit` (W30, `barrier.h:858`), `prefetch.tensormap` (W20, `copy_sm90_desc.hpp:313`), TMA loads/stores, `wait_group`, `griddepcontrol.wait`, `tcgen05.wait::ld`, `clc_decode`; absent on `elect.sync`, `barrier.cluster.*`, `commit_group`, `tcgen05.alloc/dealloc/relinquish/mma/ld`, `try_cancel`, `launch_dependents`; adding one to `tcgen05.alloc` is a harmless tightening).
